Frozen responsibility split:
  graphium.domain.document_identity
    = immutable accepted identity/load values + typed failures
  graphium.domain.document_serialization
    = pure encoding/BOM/EOL serialization policy
  graphium.infrastructure.document_loader
    = stable local filesystem byte read + observation

Explicitly deferred:
  DocumentSession/savepoint/history -> G02
  guarded physical writer / Save    -> G03
  GTK editor/Open lifecycle         -> G04
  live file monitor                 -> G11

Codec/EOL policy:
  BOM-aware UTF-8/UTF-16 LE-BE/UTF-32 LE-BE
  no BOM = strict UTF-8
  no locale fallback
  no heuristic encoding guessing
  accepted BOM retained as metadata
  source EOL facts recorded before LF normalization
  internal editor text = LF normalized
  new-document serialization default = UTF-8 / no BOM / LF
  mixed EOL = explicit normalization consent required

Filename policy:
  extension-neutral regular local text files
  no Markdown-specific file identity rule

ENTRY G01-003 — 2026-08-14 — TARGET-USER WEB RESEARCH: LEAFPAD / L3AFPAD / MOUSEPAD
================================================================================

User authorized research goal:
  identify who uses Leafpad, L3afpad and Mousepad and which Graphium features
  could realistically interest them.

Evidence basis reviewed before this entry:
  Leafpad project documentation and current issue requests;
  L3afpad project documentation and issue requests;
  Mousepad/Xfce project documentation and user discussions;
  Linux user/community discussions about lightweight GUI text editors;
  comparative lightweight-editor startup/performance material.

Observed target profile — Leafpad / L3afpad:
  quick-edit rather than IDE workflow;
  one file / one window mental model is acceptable and often preferred;
  startup immediacy and low cognitive load are central product values;
  common jobs include notes, paste/edit/save, configuration files and small text files;
  users ask for bounded convenience such as persistent word wrap, tab-width control,
  dark appearance and character counts;
  richer code-editor features such as syntax highlighting are not necessarily desired
  and can conflict with the deliberately basic product philosophy.

Observed target profile — Mousepad:
  Mousepad remains positioned as an easy, fast editor for quick text editing, but its
  feature surface can also serve a mini-code-editor audience.
  Graphium should target Mousepad's quick-edit segment, not users whose principal
  requirement is tabs, syntax highlighting or a richer code-editing environment.

Core inference for Graphium:
  FEATURE_COUNT_IS_NOT_THE_COMPETITIVE_AXIS=YES
  TARGET_AXIS=FAST_SIMPLE_SAFE
  PRIMARY_MIGRATION_SEGMENT=QUICK_EDIT_USERS

================================================================================
ENTRY G01-004 — 2026-08-14 — KILLER-FEATURE ROUTING / PRODUCT POSITIONING
================================================================================

Graphium positioning frozen from the target-user findings:
  FAST + SIMPLE + SAFE + NATIVE GTK

Killer features / differentiators, in priority order:

0. PERFORMANCE / PERCEIVED IMMEDIACY
   Speed is an admission condition, not a decorative feature. If Graphium feels
   materially slower than the lightweight-editor class, the rest of the feature
   advantage will not persuade the core Leafpad/L3afpad audience.

1. TRANSPARENT STRONG FILE SAFETY
   Preserve a small UI while adding mature behavior behind it:
     strong accepted file identity;
     changed/replaced/deleted-file awareness;
     guarded and atomic writes;
     savepoint-aware Saved/Modified state;
     alias/topology/race protection;
     no silent overwrite after external mutation.
   Safety should be mostly invisible until it prevents data loss.

2. ENCODING / BOM / EOL CLARITY
   Status/Properties should make the active representation understandable without
   forcing technical details into dialogs. Preserve representation safely and allow
   bounded explicit conversion later if authorized.

3. PERSISTENT ESSENTIAL PREFERENCES
   Graphium must remember user choices such as wrap, font, line numbers, status bar,
   tab width/spaces-tabs, appearance and useful window geometry. Minimalism does not
   mean forcing repetitive setup at every launch.

4. MATURE PRINT WORKFLOW
   Print / Print Preview / Page Setup belong to the serious desktop-editor experience.
   They must not enlarge the startup critical path.

5. COMPACT HIGH-VALUE STATUS
   Line/column, word/character count, encoding, EOL and Saved/Modified state provide
   concrete utility with very little UI surface.

6. MODERN APPEARANCE WITHOUT THEME BLOAT
   System / Light / Dark is retained. No theme marketplace/editor or cosmetic platform.

7. SAVE A COPY / SAVE VERSION COPY
   Distinctive safety/workflow value inherited from Calamus without rebinding the active
   document or polluting the simple one-file mental model.

Explicit competitive REJECT decisions remain:
  syntax highlighting in v1;
  tabs / multi-document window;
  IDE subsystems;
  project/workspace/file-browser systems;
  plugin platform.

Reason:
  Graphium should not become a smaller Geany/Kate or chase the Mousepad code-editor
  segment. Its value is mature plain-text editing with unusually strong safety.

================================================================================
ENTRY G01-005 — 2026-08-14 — PERFORMANCE & PERCEIVED LATENCY BUDGET
================================================================================

User explicitly authorized addition of:
  Performance & Perceived Latency Budget
  with permanent comparative benchmarks against Leafpad, L3afpad and Mousepad.

Canonical routing:
  Architecture Contract section 12 = binding performance contract;
  Roadmap = benchmark checkpoints and work-item routing;
  MO = rationale, history and operational interpretation.
  CANONICAL_DOCUMENT_COUNT remains exactly 3.

Permanent comparator set:
  Leafpad
  L3afpad
  Mousepad

Permanent benchmark machine for primary qualification:
  ThinkPad T480 / user's normal Linux desktop, with comparator and environment versions
  recorded in each non-canonical benchmark receipt.

Mandatory workloads from G04 onward:
  empty startup -> first editable;
  5 KiB UTF-8/LF open -> editable;
  1 MiB UTF-8/LF open -> editable;
  10 MiB UTF-8/LF open -> editable;
  idle RSS;
  RSS after 1 MiB open.

Measurement discipline:
  >=7 measured runs after one priming run for normal series;
  report median + p90 for latency;
  record comparator versions and sample hashes;
  use isolated Graphium XDG dirs;
  no root-only global cache manipulation;
  session-first/cold observations reported separately;
  no cherry-picking favorable runs.

G04 quick-editor admission ceilings:
  warm empty latency must not exceed both 2.0x Mousepad and 750 ms median;
  warm 5 KiB latency must not exceed both 2.0x Mousepad and 900 ms median;
  idle RSS <= 200 MiB.
  Failure blocks G05 unless optimized or explicitly rebaselined by user.

G12 competitive targets:
  empty + 5 KiB median target <= 1.5x Mousepad;
  1 MiB + 10 MiB median target <= 1.75x Mousepad;
  idle RSS target <= 150 MiB and <= 2.5x Mousepad where meaningful;
  Leafpad/L3afpad gaps are always published in the benchmark receipt.

Post-G04 Graphium regression budget:
  >10% median regression empty/5 KiB = closure blocker;
  >15% median regression 1 MiB/10 MiB = closure blocker;
  >15% idle-RSS growth = closure blocker unless explicitly authorized and justified.

Critical protection:
  PERFORMANCE_OPTIMIZATION_MAY_WEAKEN_SAFETY=NO
  G01-G03 correctness and guarded-write guarantees are not benchmark toggles.

Startup discipline:
  dormant Print/Preview/Page Setup, Help and optional spellcheck should be lazy;
  recent-file maintenance and live-file monitoring must avoid delaying first editability
  unless correctness requires otherwise.

ENTRY G01-009 — 2026-08-14 — GUI / HELP / SHORTCUT / DEAD-CODE MEMORANDUM
================================================================================

User requested that the following product-development memorandum be retained in the
incremental Graphium MO for routing into the appropriate future work items.

A. OPTIONAL ICON TOOLBAR / VISIBILITY
-------------------------------------
Evaluate a small native GTK toolbar (user wording: "icon bar") as a convenience layer,
not as a second command authority.

Required design questions for the future GTK-shell audit:
  1. Should View expose a checked item such as:
       [x] Toolbar
     so the toolbar can be shown/hidden without affecting menu command availability?
  2. Should toolbar visibility persist across restarts through Graphium preferences?
  3. Which commands deserve toolbar space without undermining Graphium's
     FAST + SIMPLE positioning?

Provisional product direction, NOT YET CONTRACT-FROZEN:
  - use the standard product term "Toolbar" unless mature-source audit gives a better reason;
  - one checked View menu item should control visibility;
  - hiding the toolbar must never hide/disable the corresponding menu commands;
  - visibility should be persistable once the preferences authority exists;
  - keep the toolbar deliberately small.

Provisional icon candidates for mature-source comparison:
  PRIMARY:
    New
    Open
    Save
    Undo
    Redo
    Find
  POSSIBLE:
    Print
  GENERALLY AVOID IN THE FIRST TOOLBAR UNLESS AUDIT JUSTIFIES:
    Save As
    Save a Copy
    Save Version Copy
    Cut
    Copy
    Paste
    text transformations
    preferences
    help

Reason:
  Graphium targets quick-edit users of Leafpad/L3afpad and the quick-edit segment of
  Mousepad. A crowded toolbar would conflict with the SIMPLE requirement.
  The exact icon set is explicitly deferred to direct mature-source audit when the GTK
  shell/view work item reaches this concern.

B. HIGH-QUALITY OFFLINE HELP
----------------------------
Graphium must have a good offline Help/User Guide rather than a token About dialog.

Future Help requirements:
  - explain core workflows in plain language;
  - document file-safety behavior, Saved/Modified state, external-change handling,
    encoding/BOM/EOL, printing, search/replace and preferences when those features exist;
  - document keyboard shortcuts from the same current command authority where practical,
    so Help does not silently drift away from the product;
  - update Help in the same candidate whenever a visible user-facing function is added
    or materially changed;
  - remain offline: no webview, cloud dependency or embedded browser;
  - dormant Help code/content must stay off the startup critical path.

C. KEYBOARD SHORTCUT COLLISION POLICY — LINUX MINT
---------------------------------------------------
Before freezing any new Graphium accelerator, audit likely Linux Mint / Cinnamon desktop
collisions and mature-editor conventions.

Permanent safety/usability rule:
  GRAPHIUM_SHORTCUT_COLLISION_WITH_LINUX_MINT=FORBIDDEN

Operational requirement:
  - do not assign shortcuts merely because they are unused inside Graphium;
  - compare against Linux Mint/Cinnamon global shortcuts on the T480 before desktop freeze;
  - prefer established text-editor conventions where they do not conflict;
  - menu access must remain possible even when no safe accelerator exists;
  - record intentional omissions where a desirable shortcut is reserved globally.

Known project-history warning:
  Ctrl+Alt+L is reserved by Linux Mint in the user's environment and must not be reused
  as a Graphium accelerator.

D. DEAD-CODE / UNUSED-CODE CLEANUP
----------------------------------
Graphium should remain small by removing dead or obsolete code when evidence shows that it
is truly unused.

Permanent cleanup discipline:
  - no speculative deletion based only on text search;
  - confirm call/import/reference reachability with static audit and tests;
  - distinguish compatibility/provenance helpers from genuinely dead code;
  - cleanup must preserve product behavior and architectural boundaries;
  - when cleanup is material, run full non-desktop tests and strict gates again;
  - do not accumulate unused Calamus-derived compatibility surfaces "just in case";
  - avoid cosmetic refactoring that increases churn without reducing real complexity.

Routing guidance:
  toolbar visibility/icon selection:
    route to first suitable GTK shell/view/preferences work item after mature-source audit;
  Help:
    build incrementally alongside visible features, with final closure at G12;
  shortcut collision audit:
    apply to every GTK work item that introduces or changes accelerators;
  dead-code cleanup:
    continuous audit obligation, with explicit cleanup when verified and low-risk.

No implementation is authorized merely by this memorandum.
No new canonical document is created.
G02 remains unopened by this entry.

ENTRY G02-002 — 2026-08-14 — DIRECT SOURCE + MATURE-SOURCE AUDIT
================================================================================

Exact Calamus W116 published source was inspected directly for selective extraction:
  calamus_history.py
  calamus_editor_transaction.py
  calamus_document_session.py
  calamus_document_session_controller.py
  calamus_history_runtime.py
  calamus_editor_buffer_adapter.py

Key W116 lessons retained:
  - stable non-reused editor-state IDs;
  - caret/selection snapshot separate from text dirty identity;
  - branch after Undo discards redo without reusing identity;
  - transaction checkpoint/rollback across buffer/history/session;
  - GTK adapters remain outside domain/application authority.

Mature sources already present in the source corpus were re-read for G02-specific behavior.
No new upload is required.

Scintilla UndoHistory:
  - explicit savePoint compared with currentAction;
  - branching before the save point invalidates the old positional save point;
  - BeginUndoAction/EndUndoAction group compound user operations.
  Decision: ADOPT savepoint relation and grouping semantics; ADAPT to stable Graphium
  identities rather than exposing Scintilla stack-position identity.

GNOME Text Editor / GtkTextBuffer client:
  - modified state remains distinct from externally-modified state;
  - insert and selection-bound marks are explicitly saved/restored around lifecycle work.
  Decision: ADOPT exact caret/selection restoration and separate dirty/external-change
  concepts; GTK mechanics remain deferred to G04.

gedit:
  - GtkSourceBuffer owns bounded undo and GtkTextBuffer user-action grouping;
  - document code uses begin/end user action instead of treating each low-level mutation
  as an independent command.
  Decision: ADOPT grouped-edit boundary as the future G04 adapter contract.

Geany / Scintilla family:
  - Scintilla savepoint is set after synchronization/load/save boundaries;
  - application metadata undo is layered separately from core text undo.
  Decision: ADOPT the saved-state boundary; REJECT adding metadata/history complexity to G02.

Existing Calamus W113 mature audit corroborates the same contract across Vim, Emacs,
GtkSourceView clients, Geany/Scintilla and other editors: Saved/Modified is a relation to
an exact historical state, not a sticky boolean or a text digest.

================================================================================
ENTRY G02-003 — 2026-08-14 — CONTRACT FREEZE / IMPLEMENTATION
================================================================================

G02 contract is frozen:
  G02_CONTRACT=FROZEN
  G02_SCOPE=HISTORY_TRANSACTION_SAVEPOINT_SESSION
  G02_GTK_REQUIRED=NO
  G02_PHYSICAL_WRITER=FORBIDDEN
  G02_DIRTY_AUTHORITY=EDITOR_STATE_ID_RELATION

Implementation authorities:
  graphium/domain/history.py
    bounded full-text HistoryState snapshots;
    monotonic positive state IDs, never reused;
    caret/selection refresh preserves state identity;
    branch-after-Undo receives fresh IDs;
    checkpoint/restore;
    large-document identity retained while multi-snapshot Undo is disabled.

  graphium/application/document_session.py
    one active session;
    one accepted G01 DocumentFileState;
    current_editor_state_id + saved_editor_state_id;
    Modified derived only from their relation;
    pending native text is dirty;
    exact older-state late save cannot clean newer current state;
    no filesystem write.

  graphium/application/editor_transaction.py
    GTK-free buffer port;
    New/Open history initialization;
    native-group reconciliation/commit;
    one logical programmatic transaction -> one Undo state;
    exact buffer/history/session rollback on failure;
    Undo/Redo restore text + insertion + selection bound.

Dead-code audit decision executed in G02:

Focused behavior certified by tests includes:
  - state IDs positive, monotonic and never reused after branch, pruning or rollback;
  - caret/selection-only refresh preserves text-state identity and selection direction;
  - Save A -> edit AB -> Undo A = Saved -> Redo AB = Modified;
  - Save AB -> Undo A = Modified -> Redo AB = Saved;
  - a new branch recreating identical text receives a different identity and stays Modified;
  - pending native text is immediately Modified before group commit and can reconcile cleanly
    if it nets back to the committed text;
  - late save of an older state does not clean a newer current state;
  - programmatic edit failure rolls buffer/history/session back;
  - history/session authorities still roll back if concrete-buffer rollback itself fails;
  - failed Undo restore returns all authorities to the pre-operation checkpoint;
  - Open replacement is not an undoable user edit;
  - large-document history remains bounded while current state identity and saving remain valid.

ENTRY G03-002 — 2026-08-14 — MATURE-SOURCE SAVE AUDIT / CONTRACT DECISION
================================================================================

G03 re-used the existing direct W114 mature-source audit rather than inventing a new save model.
The audit includes QtBase/QSaveFile 6.11.1, KTextEditor 6.28 and corroborating evidence from
GLib/GIO, GtkSourceView clients, GNOME Text Editor, gedit/Pluma, Geany/Scintilla, Vim,
Emacs, nano, Helix and other mature editors.

Load-bearing conclusions adopted for Graphium:
  - same-directory transactional staging is useful, but QSaveFile direct-write fallback is unsafe;
  - Graphium therefore has no silent in-place truncate/write fallback;
  - symlink logical identity must survive save; commit goes to the validated physical target;
  - hardlink replacement is semantically ambiguous and fails closed;
  - strict encoding/BOM/EOL serialization completes before target mutation;
  - stage sync error is a fatal pre-commit failure;
  - target and parent are late-revalidated before namespace commit;
  - an absent Save-As target requires a no-overwrite commit lane;
  - parent-directory fsync happens after namespace commit;
  - post-commit durability/baseline uncertainty must be reported as committed uncertainty;
  - saved editor state advances only after a real namespace commit;
  - Save As must never prebind the active DocumentSession.

Graphium deliberately does not claim true filesystem CAS against arbitrary external writers.
G11 live monitoring is not an authorization oracle for G03 Save.

No new mature-source upload was required.

================================================================================

ENTRY G04-003 — 2026-08-14 — SECOND WITHDRAWN TRANSPORT / PERFORMANCE-PROTOCOL STOP
================================================================================

Mature-source reissued transport tree:
  ac66a6b67a42f78d0398391d30f44bd973dd476e

  TESTS=157/157_PASS
  STRICT_GATES=PASS
  ENTRYPOINT_BOOTSTRAP_PREFLIGHT=PASS
  TRUE_GTK_SHELL=PASS
  TRUE_GTK_TEXTVIEW=PASS
  TRUE_GTK_OPEN_SAVE_CRLF=PASS
  TRUE_GTK_SAVEPOINT_UNDO_REDO=PASS
  TRUE_GTK_TOOLBAR_ABSENT=PASS
  SHORTCUT_COLLISION_GATE=PASS

The next performance step stopped with:
  KeyError: 'monotonic_ns'

Exact protocol diagnosis:
  the producer wrote a multi-line filesystem ready receipt through Path.write_text();
  the consumer treated mere path existence as READY. File creation can be observed after
  open/create but before all data is written, so the consumer could read a valid first line
  without the second monotonic_ns line. This is a synchronization race, not a missing field
  in the intended schema.

Classification:
  PERFORMANCE_HARNESS_PROTOCOL_INVALID=YES
  PRODUCT_TRUE_GTK_FAIL=NO
  PRODUCT_FAIL=NO
  VALID_DESKTOP_ATTEMPT=NO
  TRIAL_AND_ERROR_INCREMENT=NO
  candidate ac66a6b6...=WITHDRAWN

The stop triggered a complete G04 architecture re-audit rather than a KeyError patch.

ENTRY G04-004 — 2026-08-14 — CONFIRMATION-BIAS COUNTERMEASURE / DEEP MATURE-SOURCE REAUDIT
================================================================================

User correctly challenged the earlier audit as too shallow / potentially confirmation-biased.
G04 methodology is therefore tightened permanently.

CONFIRMATION-BIAS COUNTERMEASURE
--------------------------------
For every source comparison, record:
  1. Graphium assumption under test;
  2. mature evidence that contradicts or stresses it;
  3. materially different mature model and why it works;
  4. Graphium decision that would change if that evidence is stronger;
  5. ADOPT / ADAPT / REJECT / DEFER outcome.

An audit containing only evidence consistent with the pre-existing Graphium idea is invalid.
No new desktop candidate is issued until the whole discovered failure/design class is audited,
repaired and fully revalidated non-desktop.

Direct mature sources used for the rebuilt G04 include:
  Leafpad
  L3afpad
  Airpad
  Janus
  Mousepad 0.7.0
  Parchment
  gedit
  GNOME Text Editor
  NEdit
  JOE 4.8
  Lite XL
  published Calamus W116 where provenance is relevant

Key contradictory findings:

A. NATIVE UNDO STORAGE / GROUPING
  Earlier Graphium assumption:
    full-text G02 snapshots plus a fixed native idle/commit delay were a sufficiently simple
    quick-edit integration.

  Contradictory mature evidence:
    Leafpad/L3afpad record insert/delete deltas and coalesce structurally/key-contiguously;
    Airpad has explicit delta records and structural sequence logic;
    gedit/GNOME Text Editor/Mousepad use Gtk text/source buffer user-action boundaries for
    compound edits; NEdit and JOE use delta records, structural/contiguous grouping and
    bounded undo memory.

  Decision:
    ADAPT the common principle, not one implementation. G04 active GTK history becomes
    delta-based; `begin-user-action` / `end-user-action` and edit structure are semantic
    evidence; wall-clock time is not an Undo authority. G02 monotonic state-ID/savepoint
    semantics are preserved independently from native undo storage.

B. LARGE-DOCUMENT BEHAVIOR
  Earlier Graphium assumption:
    disabling multi-snapshot Undo above the G02 750,000-character threshold was acceptable
    safe degradation for the quick-edit target.

  Contradictory mature evidence:
    the directly inspected lightweight/mature editors store changed ranges/deltas rather than
    duplicating the complete base document per edit. A ~1 MiB text file is not exceptional
    enough to justify losing normal Undo in the target category.

  Decision:
    REJECT document-size-as-Undo-disable-switch for active G04. A 1 MiB document with one
    inserted character must keep Undo while storing one changed character of journal payload.

C. PROCESS / WINDOW TOPOLOGY
  Earlier Graphium assumption:
    one unique Gtk.Application reusing a window was a harmless implementation detail.

  Contradictory mature evidence:
    Leafpad/L3afpad naturally use independent processes/windows per invocation; Airpad
    explicitly sets G_APPLICATION_NON_UNIQUE and opens each additional command-line file in
    its own spawned process. Mousepad can disable its server but its tab/server complexity is
    intentionally outside Graphium's target.

  Decision:
    ADOPT the quick-edit topology: one invocation/process -> one window -> one document;
    Graphium is NON_UNIQUE. A second file-manager invocation cannot hijack the first document.
    Multi-file CLI input fans out one process per file.

D. PERFORMANCE ORACLE
  Earlier Graphium assumption:
    Graphium exact first-editable values could be compared to comparator first-window-mapped
    proxy values if clearly labelled.

  Contradictory mature evidence:
    Leafpad and L3afpad explicitly show the window before command-line file Open; Janus also
    shows before Open, while Airpad has a different order. FIRST_VISIBLE is therefore not
    FIRST_EDITABLE and structurally favors different startup orders.

  Decision:
    split metrics. Common FIRST_VISIBLE is measured externally by the same oracle for all four
    target products and is the only G04 cross-product ratio. Graphium exact FIRST_EDITABLE is
    a separate inherited-pipe measurement and is not compared numerically to FIRST_VISIBLE.
    G12 must establish one common external FIRST_EDITABLE oracle before hard editable-readiness
    competition claims.

E. READY SYNCHRONIZATION
  Earlier Graphium assumption:
    existence of a ready file could represent a completed readiness receipt.

  Contradictory systems fact exposed by the run:
    namespace existence and complete payload publication are different events.

  Decision:
    REJECT ready files. Graphium child emits one short complete READY record over an inherited
    pipe with one os.write; parent waits for newline and verifies emitter PID.

================================================================================
ENTRY G04-005 — 2026-08-14 — REBUILT G04 ARCHITECTURE / IMPLEMENTATION
================================================================================

G04 is rebuilt from the published G03 baseline, not patched from a withdrawn candidate.

New/changed active architecture:

  graphium.domain.edit_history.DeltaHistory
    insertion/deletion delta journal;
    positive monotonic never-reused state IDs;
    structural coalescing without timers;
    saved-state merge barrier;
    exact view-state restoration;
    bounded changed-payload storage independent of base document size.

  graphium.application.native_editor.NativeEditorController
    bridges mutable native buffer to DeltaHistory + DocumentSession;
    no full-buffer capture on ordinary native edit;
    one full capture only at explicit Save synchronization boundary;
    exact delta replay/rollback;
    Open/New are replacement boundaries and reset active undo while preserving ID monotonicity.

  graphium.application.document_session
    preserves published G02 dirty/savepoint relation;
    adds text_editor_state_id so current editor identity can advance without copying live text;
    text synchronization is explicit before physical Save.

  graphium.adapters.gtk.window
    Gtk.TextView-only classic shell;
    native insert/delete signals plus begin/end-user-action;
    no native edit commit timeout;
    no toolbar;
    File/Edit plus lazy Help menu;
    Saved/Modified title projection.

  graphium.adapters.gtk.application
    G_APPLICATION_NON_UNIQUE;
    one process/window/document;
    extra CLI files fan out to new Graphium processes.

  tools/g04_performance.py
    exact Graphium FIRST_EDITABLE over inherited pipe;
    no filesystem ready marker.

  tools/g04_comparator_proxy.py
    common external FIRST_VISIBLE oracle for Graphium/Leafpad/L3afpad/Mousepad.

  tools/g04_topology_gate.py
    desktop automated proof that separate invocations yield separate processes/windows and
    multi-file input fans out rather than becoming tabs/server-owned documents.

Visible G04 command surface:
  File: New, Open, Save, Save As, Quit
  Edit: Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste, Delete, Select All
  Help: User Guide, Keyboard Shortcuts, About

Help content is loaded only when requested and is updated in the same candidate.
Toolbar remains absent and is deferred to G06 audit.

G05_STARTED=NO

An additional hostile regression found during final harness hardening was retained rather
than weakened: if the benchmark child publishes a complete READY record and exits quickly,
the parent must drain the kernel pipe before interpreting process exit. The protocol was
corrected accordingly. This prevents a valid READY message from being discarded merely
because the child already terminated.

Performance protocol hardening also freezes:
  - exact FIRST_EDITABLE uses isolated HOME/XDG roots;
  - G04 desktop baseline is explicitly X11 because the common FIRST_VISIBLE oracle uses
    X11 window properties on the target Linux Mint/Cinnamon environment;
  - impossible child timestamps preceding parent start are rejected;
  - comparator version discovery uses only --version and never guesses -v, avoiding a
    possible GUI-producing probe that could contaminate benchmark state;
  - comparator window wait notices early process death instead of waiting the full timeout.

  GTK_DESKTOP_VALIDATION=PENDING
  PERFORMANCE_BASELINE=PENDING_T480

ENTRY G04-007 — 2026-08-14 — REBUILT CANDIDATE T480 GTK-ENVIRONMENT HARNESS STOP
================================================================================

The rebuilt G04 transport reached the T480 from the exact G03 baseline and produced:
  FULL_TESTS=183/183_PASS
  STRICT_GATES=PASS
  G04_ENTRYPOINT_BOOTSTRAP_PREFLIGHT=PASS

It then stopped before True-GTK product execution in the environment-report probe:
  AttributeError: 'gi.repository.Gtk' object has no attribute 'get_version'

Classification:
  PRODUCT_DESKTOP_EXECUTED=NO
  PRODUCT_FAIL=NO
  ATTEMPT_COUNT=NOT_APPLICABLE
  CLASSIFICATION=PRE_PRODUCT_HARNESS_API_MISMATCH

Root cause:
  the runner used a nonexistent convenience call `Gtk.get_version()` although GTK3 exposes
  major/minor/micro version accessors individually through introspection.

Repair rule:
  environment reporting is moved into a source-controlled tool and uses
  Gtk.get_major_version()/get_minor_version()/get_micro_version(). A static regression gate
  forbids reintroduction of Gtk.get_version(). No new desktop validation may be requested
  merely by changing the inline snippet.

================================================================================
ENTRY G04-008 — 2026-08-14 — SEVEN-EDITOR COMPETITIVE SYNTHESIS / PRODUCT IDENTITY FREEZE
================================================================================

Sources consolidated in this entry:
  - FeatherPad-focused audit supplied 2026-08-14;
  - `GRAPHIUM_SINTESI_COMPETITIVA_7_EDITOR_20260814.docx`, covering Leafpad, L3afpad,
    Airpad, Janus, Parchment, Mousepad and FeatherPad.

The synthesis does NOT authorize feature accumulation. It narrows Graphium's identity.

PRODUCT CATEGORY:
  LIGHTWEIGHT TRUST EDITOR

CORE POSITION:
  FAST + SIMPLE + SAFE + NATIVE GTK

PRIMARY MIGRATION SEGMENT:
  quick-edit users: Leafpad/L3afpad users and the subset of Mousepad/FeatherPad users who
  value speed, conventional plain-text maturity and low cognitive load but do not depend on
  tabs, named sessions, syntax highlighting, column editing or code-editor infrastructure.

WHAT GRAPHIUM MUST EQUAL:
  Leafpad/L3afpad:
    immediacy and low cognitive load.
  Mousepad/FeatherPad:
    ordinary desktop workflow maturity: Open/Save/Save As, search, useful status,
    preferences, printing, external-change awareness and overall polish.
  Parchment:
    discipline of scope, but NOT implicit content normalization.

WHAT GRAPHIUM MUST DO BETTER:
  1. strong file identity rather than path/mtime-only thinking;
  2. one guarded physical writer, with full-write/durability/late-revalidation semantics;
  3. exact state-ID Saved/Modified semantics through Undo/Redo/late Save;
  4. content-neutral normal Save: no silent trailing-space cleanup, final-newline insertion,
     EOL normalization, BOM conversion or encoding conversion;
  5. low-noise external-change handling where filesystem events trigger fresh strong
     observation rather than becoming truth authority;
  6. conservative representation semantics for encoding/BOM/EOL.

PERMANENT PERFORMANCE COMPARATOR SET:
  Leafpad
  L3afpad
  Mousepad
  FeatherPad

Why FeatherPad is mandatory:
  it combines very competitive launch behavior with much greater functional density.
  Therefore Graphium may not justify latency by saying that mature/lightweight editors need
  more initialization as they gain capabilities.

MINIMUM CONVENIENCE FLOOR:
  persistent Word Wrap, font, line numbers, compact status visibility, useful geometry,
  tab-width/spaces-tabs policy where relevant, System/Light/Dark, trustworthy Find/Replace,
  Recent Files, printing, conventional shortcuts and a real offline Help/User Guide.

V1 DELIBERATE REJECTIONS:
  tabs in one window;
  named sessions/workspaces as primary workflow;
  syntax/language-mode infrastructure;
  column editing;
  plugin platform/marketplace;
  project/file-browser/IDE shell;
  binary/hex editing as product identity;
  complex theme platform.

These are not missing features. They protect the product category and startup/state budget.

================================================================================
ENTRY G04-009 — 2026-08-14 — ROADMAP / PERFORMANCE / TRUST-PROPOSITION REBASELINE
================================================================================

The seven-editor synthesis is routed into the existing canonical roadmap and architecture
contract without creating a fourth canonical authority.

Frozen strategic decisions:
  FEATURE_COUNT_IS_NOT_THE_COMPETITIVE_AXIS=YES
  PRODUCT_CATEGORY=LIGHTWEIGHT_TRUST_EDITOR
  TARGET_AXIS=FAST+SIMPLE+SAFE+NATIVE_GTK
  PRIMARY_MIGRATION_SEGMENT=QUICK_EDIT_USERS
  PERMANENT_PERFORMANCE_COMPARATORS=Leafpad,L3afpad,Mousepad,FeatherPad
  NORMAL_SAVE_IS_CONTENT_NEUTRAL=YES
  PHYSICAL_WRITER_AUTHORITY_COUNT=1
  FILE_MONITOR_IS_OBSERVATION_TRIGGER_NOT_TRUTH_AUTHORITY=YES
  V1_TABS_SYNTAX_IDE_PLUGIN_PLATFORM=NO
  SAFETY_AND_PERFORMANCE_MAY_NOT_WEAKEN_EACH_OTHER=YES

Roadmap consequences:
  G04:
    first desktop performance baseline includes FeatherPad in the common FIRST_VISIBLE set;
    exact Graphium FIRST_EDITABLE remains separate until a common external editable oracle
    exists for all products.
  G05:
    Find/Replace correctness is trust infrastructure, including Unicode and grouped Undo.
  G06:
    delivers the small view/status conveniences expected by lightweight-editor users;
    word/character counts are allowed only if cheap enough not to create eager whole-buffer
    work. Toolbar remains an audit decision, not an assumed feature.
  G08:
    Print/Preview/Page Setup must reach mature desktop quality while remaining lazy.
  G10:
    persists only high-utility recurring preferences and appearance choices.
  G11:
    Mousepad is the positive monitoring/lifecycle comparator; FeatherPad is a contrast oracle
    for weaker mtime-oriented external-change/persistence semantics. Mandatory hostile case:
      accept A -> external process replaces A with B -> Save must not silently overwrite B.
  G12:
    competitive proof is against all four permanent comparators and must demonstrate FAST +
    SIMPLE + SAFE rather than assert it.
  G13:
    post-v1 recovery remains a crash-recovery cache separate from the target, not traditional
    background autosave authority.

Competitive success criteria at v1 closure:
  1. ordinary quick edit is not sensibly described as slow by the lightweight target user;
  2. common Open/Save/Search/Print/preferences workflows feel mature despite smaller scope;
  3. Normal Save never silently normalizes content;
  4. stale/replaced accepted targets cannot be silently overwritten;
  5. Saved/Modified remains exact through Undo/Redo/programmatic edits/late Save;
  6. persistent conveniences eliminate repeated setup without preference-platform bloat;
  7. monitoring protects without routine false-positive warnings;
  8. Graphium remains recognizably a plain-text quick editor, not a reduced IDE;
  9. performance qualification uses Leafpad/L3afpad/Mousepad/FeatherPad on the real desktop;
 10. strongest safety mechanisms stay invisible on the happy path and surface only when they
     prevent ambiguity or data loss.

G04 remains OPEN.
G05_STARTED=NO
CANONICAL_DOCUMENT_COUNT=3

ENTRY G04-011 — 2026-08-14 — VALID MANUAL PRODUCT FAIL: 1 MiB SINGLE-LINE RESPONSIVENESS
================================================================================

During final human desktop validation, the user reported a FAIL on the large-file step:
`one-mib.txt`, Ctrl+End, type Z initially appeared usable with Undo available, but the
Graphium window subsequently hung.

Classification:
  CLASSIFICATION=VALID_MANUAL_PRODUCT_FAIL
  PRODUCT_FAIL=YES
  FAILURE_DOMAIN=INTERACTIVE_RESPONSIVENESS
  G04_CANDIDATE_TREE=caa1dc06f3e937a14ceb1499bbe3a9f73d1b6260
  CANDIDATE_STATUS=WITHDRAWN
  G04_REMAINS_OPEN=YES
  G05_STARTED=NO

Exact fixture audit found that `one-mib.txt` was generated as one uninterrupted string of
1,048,576 `a` characters. It was therefore a pathological single logical line, not a
representative 1 MiB multiline text document. The automated True-GTK gate used the same
shape but never reproduced the user's real viewport navigation to the line end, so an
oracle-coverage defect coexisted with the real product failure.

The delta-history redesign is NOT rolled back: Undo stayed available and stored only the
changed payload. The new evidence instead falsifies the assumption that delta-history
scalability alone proves interactive renderability of every document shape.

No further manual group is certified from this validation. No patch-and-retry candidate is
authorized by the failure itself; mature-source re-audit of the whole renderer-safety class
is required first.

================================================================================
ENTRY G04-012 — 2026-08-14 — HUGE-LINE MATURE-SOURCE REAUDIT / RENDERABILITY CONTRACT
================================================================================

Failure-specific mature-source audit is recorded in:
  evidence/G04_HUGE_LINE_PRODUCT_FAIL_REAUDIT.txt

Load-bearing findings:
  - GNOME GtkTextView/GtkSourceView issue history documents severe responsiveness problems
    for very long logical lines and explicitly discusses 20,000-column lines as a design
    stress case.
  - FeatherPad source applies an explicit huge-line guard around 500,000 characters, but
    mutates the displayed text with a truncation marker and forces uneditable state.
  - NEdit's custom display layer freezes MAX_DISP_LINE_LEN=1000 for rendering work.
  - JOE disables expensive syntax/context presentation on very large files.
  - Leafpad/L3afpad/Airpad expose plain GtkTextView/no-wrap paths without an explicit guard
    in inspected source and are therefore not positive oracles for this failure class.
  - Mousepad uses GtkSourceView and provides no basis for claiming that plain GtkTextView
    safely handles million-character lines.

Graphium decision:
  ADOPT explicit renderer-safety admission.
  ADAPT to the actual GtkTextView product with an initial conservative 20,000 Unicode-char
        logical-line budget.
  REJECT FeatherPad-style truncation/marker insertion because Normal Open must be content
        neutral.
  REJECT read-only fallback in the same GtkTextView because rendering/navigation itself is
        the observed failure class.
  DEFER exact paged/streamed viewing to separate future architecture.

Frozen semantics:
  G04_INTERACTIVE_LINE_BUDGET_CHARS=20000
  G04_PATHOLOGICAL_LINE_POLICY=REFUSE_BEFORE_GTK_BUFFER_INSTALL
  G04_PATHOLOGICAL_LINE_CONTENT_MUTATION=FORBIDDEN
  LARGE_FILE_SIZE_AND_HUGE_LINE_WIDTH_ARE_SEPARATE_AXES=YES

The 20,000 value is an initial Graphium product safety budget for its chosen renderer. It is
not asserted to be a universal GTK hard limit and may be revised only by explicit later
renderer qualification.

Repair scope covers the whole class:
  1. pre-install Open renderability guard;
  2. source/current-document bytes never mutated by refusal;
  3. insertion/paste preflight before GtkTextBuffer default mutation;
  4. newline-deleting join preflight before default mutation;
  5. blocked edit does not advance state identity/history and shows warning;
  6. True-GTK large-file test becomes realistic multiline + actual end scrolling;
  7. True-GTK separately verifies huge-line Open and paste refusal;
  8. human large-file test uses realistic multiline text rather than a manufactured
     million-character line.

================================================================================
ENTRY G04-013 — 2026-08-14 — RENDERER-SAFETY REPAIR NONDESKTOP VALIDATED / DESKTOP REQUALIFICATION READY

  exact 20,000-character boundary accepted;
  20,001 rejected;
  realistic large multiline document accepted;

  insert/paste overflow rejected;
  multi-line inserted fragments checked independently;
  newline-deleting join overflow rejected;

  failed huge-line Open preserves buffer/session/history and source bytes exactly;

ENTRY G04-012 — 2026-08-14 — MENU ARCHITECTURE R3 / COMPETITIVE SURFACE FREEZE
================================================================================

Authorization:
  User authorized freezing the reviewed Graphium menu proposal as Menu
  Architecture R3 and routing its product consequences into the canonical MO
  and G05-G12 roadmap. No G04 product/runtime implementation is authorized by
  this documentation step; G04 remains OPEN and G05 remains NOT STARTED.

Competitive conclusion:
  The six-menu architecture is ADOPTED because it does not change Graphium's
  product category when duplication and diagnostic prominence are removed.
  Graphium is not required to have the fewest commands. It is required to have
  the smallest conventional desktop command surface that prevents the target
  quick-edit user from feeling that a real editor is missing, while keeping
  tabs/sessions/syntax/projects/plugins outside v1.

Frozen top-level menu architecture:
  TOP_LEVEL_MENUS=File,Edit,Search,View,Document,Help
  TOOLS_MENU=NO
  WINDOW_MENU=NO
  FORMAT_MENU=NO
  LANGUAGE_MENU=NO
  SESSION_MENU=NO
  PLUGINS_MENU=NO

Frozen semantic ownership:
  File      = persistence + document/file lifecycle
  Edit      = local text editing + preferences that have no direct command
  Search    = content search/navigation
  View      = how the active text is presented
  Document  = representation/state facts and explicit representation conversion
  Help      = user understanding + compact support information

R3 command routing:

  FILE
    New
    Open…
    Open Recent >
    Save
    Save As…
    Save a Copy…
    Save Version Copy…
    Reload from Disk
    Properties…
    Page Setup…
    Print Preview
    Print…
    Quit

    Close is REJECTED for v1. With one process/window/document, New already
    replaces the active document after lifecycle protection, while Quit closes
    the process. A separate Close command would add lifecycle ambiguity without
    a distinct user outcome.

  EDIT
    Undo
    Redo
    Cut
    Copy
    Paste
    Delete
    Select All
    Preferences…

    Paste as Plain Text is REJECTED for v1: Graphium is already a plain-text
    editor and ordinary Paste owns text insertion semantics.

  SEARCH
    Find…
    Find Next
    Find Previous
    Replace…
    Go to Line…

    Find Next/Previous are first-class commands, not UI-private button actions.
    Regex/fuzzy/multi-file search remain outside the v1 MUST surface.

  VIEW
    Status Bar
    Line Numbers
    Word Wrap
    Font…
    Zoom In
    Zoom Out
    Reset Zoom
    Appearance > System / Light / Dark
    Full Screen
    Toolbar = DEFER to G06 mature-source/target-user audit

    Font owns configured family+size. Zoom is a transient/current magnification
    relative to that configured font and Reset Zoom means 100%, not reset font.
    View toggles/preferences are persistent but are not duplicated inside the
    Preferences dialog.

  DOCUMENT
    Encoding >
    Line Endings >
    Statistics…

    Encoding and Line Endings must distinguish OBSERVATION from CONVERSION.
    Current representation is displayed as a fact. A user-requested change is
    labelled/treated as an explicit Convert action. Ordinary Save never becomes
    an implicit representation conversion command.

    Indentation is REJECTED from Document because tab width and tabs/spaces are
    editor behavior/preferences, not document representation facts.

    Statistics is on-demand. Mandatory live word/character counting in the
    status bar is REJECTED unless later evidence proves an incremental/cheap
    implementation that does not violate performance budgets.

  HELP
    Graphium Help
    Keyboard Shortcuts
    About Graphium

    System Information is ADAPTED into About Graphium (with copyable support
    details if useful) rather than a separate top-level Help command.

Preferences anti-duplication rule:
  DIRECT_COMMAND_SETTING_DUPLICATION=FORBIDDEN
  A persistent setting with a clear direct command surface is changed there and
  remembered there. Preferences does not repeat Word Wrap, Line Numbers,
  Status Bar, Toolbar, Appearance or Font merely to create a second route.

  Initial Preferences ownership:
    Tab width
    Insert spaces for tabs
    future settings only when they have no clearer direct command

  Safety policy is never configurable as a cosmetic preference. No option may
  disable guarded Save, ignore external mutation, or weaken strong identity.

Properties / disk-state routing:
  Properties remains a central File command and is the compact place where
  Graphium can expose serious document/file facts without polluting the main UI:
    logical name/location
    size / modification observation
    encoding / BOM / EOL
    Saved / Modified
    writable state
    symlink / hard-link facts at an appropriate user level

  The proposed `Check File on Disk` capability is ADOPTED as functionality but
  REJECTED as a permanent primary Document-menu item. It is routed to
  Properties as an explicit `Check Now` action and to G11 automatic monitoring.
  The automatic monitor remains interrupt-only; a fresh strong observation is
  the truth authority.

Status-bar freeze:
  v1 compact core = line/column + encoding/EOL + Saved/Modified.
  live word/character count = OPTIONAL only after cheapness proof.
  document-wide counts remain available on demand through Statistics… .

Toolbar freeze:
  TOOLBAR_G04=ABSENT
  TOOLBAR_G06=DEFER_UNTIL_AUDIT
  If G06 adopts a toolbar, it is optional, default-off is the leading hypothesis,
  visibility is persistent, and the command set stays deliberately small.
  No toolbar implementation is authorized by this R3 freeze.

Anti-bloat decision:
  MENU_COUNT_IS_NOT_BLOAT_METRIC=YES
  DUPLICATED_STATE_SURFACES_ARE_BLOAT=YES
  DIAGNOSTIC_PROMINENCE_IS_BLOAT_WHEN_NOT_WORKFLOW_CRITICAL=YES
  COMMAND_MUST_HELP_NORMAL_TEXT_EDITING_FASTER_CLEARER_OR_SAFER=YES

  The adopted R3 surface is intended to be:
    more operationally mature than Leafpad/L3afpad;
    simpler and lower-state than Mousepad/FeatherPad;
    less content-prescriptive than Parchment;
    more rigorous than the comparator set on file identity/persistence semantics.

Roadmap routing:
  G05 = Search menu command authority / trustworthy Find-Replace-Go-to-Line.
  G06 = View menu core, compact status, Font/Zoom/Fullscreen, toolbar audit.
  G07 = Open Recent, Save a Copy, Save Version Copy, Properties + manual Check Now.
  G08 = Page Setup / Print Preview / Print, lazy.
  G09 = explicit transformations only; they do not require a Format top-level menu.
  G10 = persistence layer/preferences dialog without duplicating direct View commands.
  G11 = Reload/external-change semantics + automatic strong re-observation.
  G12 = full six-menu consistency, Help/shortcut synchronization and anti-bloat closure.

Canonical-document count after this update remains exactly 3.


===============================================================================
2026-08-14 — LIGHTWEIGHT BUDGET / MANDATORY ANTI-BLOAT GATE FOR EVERY Gxx
===============================================================================

Decision status:
  FROZEN PRODUCT/METHODOLOGY RULE
  APPLIES_TO=G05_AND_ALL_LATER_Gxx
  DOES_NOT_CHANGE_G04_PRODUCT_SCOPE=YES
  G04_STATUS=OPEN
  G05_STARTED=NO

Reason for this freeze:
  Menu Architecture R3 is a MAXIMUM V1 ENVELOPE, not an implementation checklist.
  A function being useful is not sufficient reason to add it. Every proposed
  capability must also justify its permanent cost in product state, runtime work,
  architecture, testing, Help, persistence and user mental model.

Core lightweight principle:
  Graphium remains lightweight when it gives the user the smallest coherent set
  of mature desktop-text-editor capabilities needed for normal quick editing,
  while refusing infrastructure whose main purpose is multi-document management,
  software development, project organization or extensibility.

  The controlling question for every visible command remains:

    DOES THIS HELP THE USER EDIT A NORMAL TEXT FILE
    FASTER, CLEARER OR MORE SAFELY?

  If the answer is YES, the feature is only a candidate for adoption. It must
  still pass the Lightweight Budget below.

LIGHTWEIGHT_BUDGET_MANDATORY_GATE=YES
MENU_ARCHITECTURE_R3_IS_MAXIMUM_ENVELOPE_NOT_CHECKLIST=YES
FEATURE_UTILITY_ALONE_IS_INSUFFICIENT=YES

Mandatory anti-bloat review for each future Gxx feature:

  1. USER VALUE
     - Is the capability directly useful to the primary quick-edit target?
     - Is the benefit frequent or important enough to justify permanent surface?
     - Does it improve speed, clarity or safety of ordinary text-file work?

  2. MENTAL-MODEL COST
     - Does it introduce a new concept the user must learn?
     - Can it reuse an existing File/Edit/Search/View/Document/Help concept?
     - Does it create overlapping commands or two ways to configure the same fact?

  3. PERSISTENT-STATE COST
     - Does it require new persistent state?
     - If yes, is that state small, explicit, filesystem-backed or ordinary config,
       and clearly owned by one authority?
     - New hidden databases, session stores or feature-specific registries are
       forbidden unless a later explicitly authorized work item proves necessity.

  4. BACKGROUND-WORK COST
     - Background activity is not justified merely by convenience.
     - No background analytics, document-wide polling or periodic rescans.
     - File monitoring, where required, remains event/interrupt driven and its
       event is never the truth authority.
     - Work that can be on-demand or lazy must remain on-demand or lazy.

  5. STARTUP-CRITICAL-PATH COST
     - Printing, Help, Statistics, monitoring maintenance and other nonessential
       subsystems must stay outside startup critical path unless proven otherwise.
     - No eager syntax/parser/analytics initialization.
     - Safety may not be weakened to improve benchmarks; performance may not be
       ignored merely because a feature is useful.

  6. ARCHITECTURAL COST
     - A marginal command does not justify a new subsystem, service graph,
       state machine or generalized framework.
     - Prefer a small direct implementation with one clear authority.
     - If a feature requires architecture that mainly exists to support itself,
       default decision is DEFER or REJECT.

  7. DUPLICATION COST
     - DIRECT_COMMAND_SETTING_DUPLICATION remains FORBIDDEN.
     - A persistent View setting controlled directly from View is not repeated in
       Preferences simply to expose a second route.
     - Diagnostic information belongs in Properties/About unless it is itself a
       normal workflow action.

  8. DOCUMENT-WIDE COMPUTATION COST
     - No mandatory eager whole-document analytics for status decoration.
     - Word/character statistics remain on-demand unless a later implementation
       proves incremental/cheap behavior under real performance gates.
     - Large-document and pathological-line safety remains part of qualification.

  9. STORAGE / RECOVERY COST
     - No session restoration or multi-document workspace state in v1.
     - Future crash recovery, if adopted at G13, uses a separate recovery cache and
       does not silently grant a background subsystem authority to Save the target.

  10. SCOPE-DRIFT CHECK
      The following remain outside the v1 identity and are not reintroduced by
      incremental convenience work:
        NO tabs
        NO named sessions
        NO workspace/project tree
        NO syntax/language engine
        NO column-editing platform
        NO plugin/extensibility platform
        NO IDE/LSP/debugger/terminal/Git subsystem
        NO multi-file search workflow
        NO binary/hex-editor identity
        NO theme marketplace / arbitrary styling platform

  11. HELP / TEST / MAINTENANCE COST
      - Every visible command must update Help/Keyboard Shortcuts in the same work
        item when applicable.
      - Every new persistent state requires regression coverage for defaults,
        persistence and failure behavior.
      - Every file-affecting feature must preserve strong identity, guarded Save,
        content neutrality and exact Saved/Modified semantics.

  12. REAL-DESKTOP COST
      - A feature that passes headless tests but makes the T480 shell noticeably
        slower, less responsive or visually more complex may still be rejected.
      - Performance and responsiveness qualification remains product behavior, not
        a cosmetic optimization.

Decision matrix required for each candidate feature:

  ADOPT
    High/clear target-user value; bounded state and runtime cost; no scope drift;
    no duplicated surface; passes safety/performance requirements.

  ADAPT
    Useful capability, but mature-source form is too large or stateful; Graphium
    implements a deliberately smaller form that preserves the user benefit.

  DEFER
    Useful but nonessential for the current v1, or its implementation cost cannot
    yet be shown to fit the Lightweight Budget.

  REJECT
    Primarily serves multi-document/code/project/extensibility workflows, creates
    disproportionate infrastructure, duplicates state/surface, weakens safety or
    performance, or changes Graphium's product category.

Current R3 examples under this rule:

  Toolbar
    DEFER to G06 audit. Presence in the maximum envelope is not authorization.

  Full Screen
    ADOPT only if implementation is near-zero architectural/runtime cost and does
    not create a separate Zen-mode subsystem.

  Statistics
    ADAPT as on-demand dialog. Mandatory live whole-document counting is not part
    of v1 without cheapness proof.

  Properties
    ADOPT as compact fact surface; REJECT inspector/debug-console expansion.

  Open Recent
    ADOPT as simple file history; REJECT session/workspace semantics.

  Save Version Copy
    ADOPT only as deterministic copy command; REJECT timeline/database/versioning
    subsystem growth.

  Encoding / Line Endings
    ADOPT as observation + explicit conversion; REJECT implicit Save conversion,
    broad codec/platform expansion for its own sake or content normalization.

  Preferences
    ADAPT as intentionally small. It owns only settings without a clearer direct
    command surface; it does not become a second copy of View/Document menus.

Lightweight closure criterion for each Gxx:

  A work item cannot be considered competitively closed merely because its tests
  pass. Its final audit must also record that the implemented delta remains within
  the Lightweight Budget and did not create hidden scope expansion.

  Required closing marker:

    LIGHTWEIGHT_BUDGET_GATE=PASS

  A PASS means, at minimum:
    startup critical path not unnecessarily expanded;
    no duplicate settings/command authority introduced;
    no new hidden persistent database/session state;
    no unjustified background work;
    no scope drift toward tabs/syntax/projects/plugins;
    no weakening of file-safety semantics;
    no accepted responsiveness regression without explicit product decision.

Strategic interpretation:
  Graphium must finish v1 feeling like:

    "It has everything I need to edit an ordinary text file."

  It must not finish v1 feeling like:

    "It has almost everything Mousepad or FeatherPad has."

  Therefore LESS remains an active design tool. A mature-source feature may be
  useful and still be deliberately omitted when omission better protects the
  quick-edit workflow, startup responsiveness, low-state architecture or safety.

Canonical-document count remains exactly 3.

================================================================================
ENTRY — G04 FEATHERPAD COMPARATOR PROCESS-ISOLATION HARNESS DEFECT
Date: 2026-08-14
================================================================================

Observed during renderer-safety desktop requalification after Graphium itself had passed:
- 195/195 non-desktop tests in the tested transport;
- strict gates;
- True-GTK renderer-safety gate;
- one-process/one-window/one-document topology;
- Linux Mint shortcut audit;
- exact Graphium FIRST_EDITABLE admission.

The common FIRST_VISIBLE run completed Graphium, Leafpad, L3afpad and Mousepad, then a FeatherPad window appeared but the oracle stopped with:

  RuntimeError: no new X11 top-level window for pid <spawned-pid>: ['/usr/bin/featherpad']

Classification:
  PRE_MANUAL_COMPARATOR_HARNESS_DEFECT
  GRAPHIUM_PRODUCT_FAIL=NO
  GRAPHIUM_PRODUCT_RUNTIME_CHANGED_BY_REPAIR=NO

Root cause:
FeatherPad is single-instance by default. Its normal launch can transfer/open the requested window in an already-running FeatherPad process, while the common oracle intentionally requires the new X11 top-level to belong to the exact PID spawned for that measured run. Accepting an arbitrary FeatherPad-owned window would weaken the common metric and make process-start attribution ambiguous.

Repair decision:
- preserve exact spawned-PID ownership;
- preserve common external FIRST_VISIBLE definition;
- Mousepad continues to use `--disable-server` when supported;
- FeatherPad MUST use `--standalone`;
- if an installed FeatherPad build lacks `--standalone`, comparative qualification is BLOCKED rather than weakening the oracle;
- add a regression gate proving both process-isolation flags and exact-PID ownership remain present.

Methodological principle:
  FIX COMPARATOR TOPOLOGY; DO NOT RELAX THE ORACLE.

After adding the regression test, the non-desktop suite authority becomes 196 tests for the repaired transport.

G04 remains OPEN. Desktop requalification must restart from the repaired harness package because comparative receipts from the stopped run are incomplete. G05 remains NOT STARTED.

Root cause:
The packaging step copied a post-test working directory rather than a source-clean tree.
Graphium's `.gitignore` already excludes `__pycache__/`, `*.pyc` and equivalent generated

================================================================================
ENTRY — G05 SEARCH SCALE HARDENING / LIGHTWEIGHT BUDGET
Date: 2026-08-14
================================================================================

During pre-candidate implementation hardening, two hidden scale costs were found before
desktop qualification:

1. The first Unicode casefold offset map retained document-wide boundary information and
   produced unacceptable cost on a 10 MiB document containing a length-changing fold.
2. Find Next/Previous initially called find_all(), so a frequent one-character query could
   materialize millions of SearchMatch objects even though navigation needs only one match.

These are classified as PRE-CANDIDATE ARCHITECTURAL/PERFORMANCE DEFECTS, not desktop
product failures. No user Git mutation or desktop candidate had occurred.

Frozen repair:
- Unicode case-insensitive matching remains full casefold-correct, including exact mapping
  of length-changing folds such as ß -> ss;
- because query/replacement are single-line and G04 bounds interactive logical-line width,
  casefold/map working memory is LOGICAL-LINE-BOUNDED rather than document-wide;
- Find Next/Previous use direct forward/backward search and never materialize all matches;
- Replace All alone enumerates its frozen original-source match set;
- Replace All match materialization cap = 50,000; the 50,001st match fails closed before
  final-text/replay-plan construction;
- DeltaHistory changed-payload budget remains a separate authoritative pre-mutation bound;
- case-sensitive query==replacement is a provable no-op and returns without dense scanning.

Local engineering measurement after the line-bounded redesign (not T480 certification):
- 10 MiB case-sensitive Find Next to end: approximately 6 ms;
- 10 MiB case-insensitive Find Next to end: approximately 250 ms;
- inserting one ß expansion into the 10 MiB fixture remains approximately 250 ms rather
  than the approximately 6.8 s observed in the rejected document-wide mapping design.

Markers:
  G05_CASEFOLD_WORKING_SET=LOGICAL_LINE_BOUNDED
  G05_FIND_NAVIGATION_MATERIALIZES_ALL_MATCHES=NO
  G05_REPLACE_ALL_MATCH_CAP=50000
  G05_REPLACE_ALL_MATCH_CAP_FAILURE=PRE_MUTATION_FAIL_CLOSED
  G05_DESKTOP_CANDIDATE=NO
  GIT_MUTATION=NO_USER_REPO

This hardening is a direct application of the permanent Lightweight Budget gate.

2026-08-14 — G05 PRE-CANDIDATE NONDESKTOP CLOSURE
- Post-implementation source/dead-code audit PASS; no duplicate search/programmatic-edit authority.
- Full suite 238/238 PASS.
- Five consecutive strict verifier runs PASS.
- Latest local engineering g05_search_performance.py PASS (NOT T480 certification):
  find-cs-1m 0.379 ms; find-ci-1m 11.913 ms; find-ci-expansion-1m 12.343 ms;
  find-cs-10m 4.784 ms; find-ci-10m 120.280 ms; find-ci-expansion-10m 131.728 ms;
  replace-all-1m 5.673 ms; replace-all-10m 61.962 ms; replace-cap-refusal 35.827 ms;
  maximum observed worker RSS 164.02 MiB.
- G05 remains OPEN; desktop qualification pending; no user-repository Git mutation.

  attempted to open another fixture;
- published Graphium lifecycle correctly opened the real synchronous unsaved-changes dialog;
- the gate owned no response, so the outer 90s watchdog eventually stopped it.

Mature-source closure from Mousepad 0.7.0 + Leafpad + L3afpad and published Graphium G04/G05:
- modal Gtk lifecycle is synchronously blocking by design;
- a test that expects a dialog must schedule response ownership BEFORE triggering it;
- a test that does not expect a dialog must preserve an exact Saved lifecycle boundary;
- unexpected dialogs in a non-modal gate are unwound only to escape the nested loop and then
  FAIL the gate; generic auto-cancel-and-continue is forbidden;
- every GLib timeout/source has explicit ownership/cleanup;
- outer process timeout is containment only, never semantic dialog authority.

Static harness hardening only; Graphium runtime unchanged:
- exact state-ID clean-boundary assertions;
- real Undo closes the Compact Status mutation scenario;
- clean-only fixture Open helper;
- unexpected-modal tripwire around modal-capable calls;
- View performance fixture transitions also require exact clean lifecycle state.

Future qualification topology:
- NO integrated-checkpoint R3;
- eventual G06 desktop candidate uses independent fresh-process lanes for G04 regression,
  G05 regression, G06 View semantics, G06 View performance, topology/shortcuts, startup,
  four-comparator FIRST_VISIBLE, Search regression/performance and startup self-regression;
- manual View acceptance begins only after every automated lane passes.

ENTRY — G07 R1 T480 NON-CANDIDATE STARTUP REGRESSION / DIRECT MATURE RE-AUDIT / REPAIR
Date: 2026-08-16
================================================================================

Fresh T480 NON-CANDIDATE qualification proved all functional G04-G07 True-GTK, View, Statistics, topology, shortcut and Search lanes PASS, but startup self-regression failed on FIRST_EDITABLE 5KiB and FIRST_VISIBLE 1MiB. No candidate attempt was consumed. Exact R1 source audit isolated a fixed post-Open cost: RecentFilesController.touch() synchronously persisted the MRU using both file fsync and parent-directory fsync.

Failure-specific direct mature-source re-audit used only uploaded/local sources, never web evidence. FeatherPad mutates recentFiles_ in memory and writes config at teardown; GNOME Text Editor records seen files in memory and persists later through session save; Mousepad and gedit delegate recent addition after successful Open without product-owned fsync barriers; NEdit is the strongest synchronous contrast and still uses only fopen/fprintf/fclose; Leafpad/L3afpad have no Recent subsystem. Mature evidence therefore rejects document-grade durability for MRU convenience state.

Frozen user semantics remain unchanged. Repair clarification:
  G07_RECENT_DURABILITY=ATOMIC_CONVENIENCE_NO_FSYNC
  retain 0600 temporary JSON + close + atomic os.replace
  remove Recent file fsync and directory fsync only
  GuardedFileWriter/document Save durability remains untouched
  no thread, timer, idle service, GtkRecentManager, XBEL or session manager introduced

Repair remains NON-CANDIDATE until a fresh T480 startup qualification passes.
  G07_R1_T480_FUNCTIONAL_TRUE_GTK=PASS
  G07_R1_STARTUP_SELF_REGRESSION=FAIL
  G07_R1_REPAIR_STATUS=BUILT_ISOLATED_PENDING_REQUALIFICATION
  CANDIDATE_ATTEMPT_CONSUMED=NO
  GIT_MUTATION=NO_USER_REPO

2026-08-17 — G07 comparator-stop mature-source re-audit
A NON-CANDIDATE startup-repair rerun stopped only when Leafpad failed once to expose a new X11 top-level
for its exact spawned PID. Direct source audit confirms Leafpad/L3afpad direct window ownership,
Mousepad --disable-server -> G_APPLICATION_NON_UNIQUE, and FeatherPad --standalone process ownership.
No Graphium runtime repair is justified. The G04 comparator harness is hardened to wait for X11
quiescence after each exited measured PID, preserve partial receipts, classify launch/window anomalies
as comparator blocks (rc=3) with diagnostics, and never silently retry a failed sample. The G06 startup
anti-bloat self-gate is decoupled from competitor completeness; comparative receipt completeness remains
mandatory independently. Mature-source-before-repair rule reaffirmed.

ENTRY — G07 R1 REISSUE GDK NAMESPACE HARNESS STOP / DIRECT MATURE-SOURCE REAUDIT
Date: 2026-08-18

The first contamination-aware R1 Reissue passed 304/304 + strict and G04/G05/G06/G07 True-GTK, then stopped before the first View-performance product measurement because the newly added harness tripwire imported Gdk before a major version had been frozen. On the T480 PyGObject loaded Gdk 4.0 and Gtk3 then rejected the mixed namespace.

Failure-specific direct source audit used only the preserved mature corpus. L3afpad, Mousepad and gedit bind a coherent GTK3/GDK3 family; GNOME Text Editor binds a coherent GTK4/GDK4 family; FeatherPad is coherently Qt. The defect was therefore qualification namespace ownership, not Graphium runtime. The worker-only repair requires both `gi.require_version("Gdk", "3.0")` and `gi.require_version("Gtk", "3.0")` before importing either namespace.

  must prove published G08 parent commit/tree, clean synchronized canonical main, reconstruct the exact

================================================================================
ENTRY — GS07 ANTI-COSMETIC STRUCTURAL SIMPLIFICATION GOVERNANCE — BINDING
Date: 2026-08-22
State: CANONICAL / BINDING / IMMEDIATE EFFECT
================================================================================

PURPOSE
-------
Graphium structural simplification MUST reduce accidental complexity in measurable physical and
architectural terms. A change is NOT a simplification merely because files, test names, helpers,
profiles or orchestration have been renamed, moved, wrapped, grouped or encapsulated.

This governance is binding for GS07 and for every future Graphium structural-simplification line.
It supplements, and where necessary tightens, the earlier GS01-GS06 simplification design.
It does NOT lower any essential product-safety, filesystem-safety, GTK lifecycle, identity,
savepoint/dirty-state, rendering, printing, performance or desktop-certification guarantee.
Mature-source comparison remains mandatory for every substantive simplification decision.

IMMEDIATE FREEZE CONSEQUENCE
----------------------------
G10 remains FROZEN/PENDING.
G10 Candidate R1 remains NOT DECLARED.
G10 candidate attempts remain 0/2.
No new G10 product repair, probe, pre-candidate requalification or Candidate construction is
permitted until GS07 completes both functional rebaseline and the structural reduction gates below.

The existing GS07 old-vs-new T480 rebaseline runner remains useful and REQUIRED for functional
shadow equivalence, but its PASS alone is explicitly INSUFFICIENT to close GS07 or unfreeze G10.

BINDING FINAL GATE
------------------
GS07 and every future simplification stage MUST end with:

  STRUCTURAL_SIMPLIFICATION_IS_NET_REDUCTIVE=PASS

If this marker cannot be proved from measured evidence, the simplification stage is FAIL even if
all product, unit, integration, True-GTK, performance and release tests pass.

MANDATORY STRUCTURAL GATES
--------------------------

1. NET DELETION GATE
   - Active validation/release infrastructure MUST materially shrink.
   - Deleting 2,000 lines from historical Gxx files and adding approximately the same amount under
     semantic names is NOT a PASS.
   - Files and LOC moved, renamed, generated, wrapped or hidden behind compatibility layers count as
     retained complexity, not deleted complexity.
   - For GS07, active validation Python LOC MUST be at least 50% below the published-G09 active
     validation baseline (~9,557 LOC), unless a later source-grounded audit proves that this exact
     baseline measure was wrong.
   - The current ~3,681 LOC GS07 prototype is only preliminary evidence; the final active tree must
     reproduce the measurement.

2. AUTHORITY-COUNT GATE
   - The number of independent authorities able to decide the same fact MUST decrease.
   - One current behavior/invariant MUST have one permanent owner.
   - Behavioral, Integration/Filesystem, True-GTK Desktop and Packaging/Release are the four
     permanent qualification authorities.
   - Candidate/R1/R2/attempt/commit/tree/publication state remain external release-engineering state
     and MUST NOT become a fifth product authority.

3. EXECUTABLE-HISTORY GATE
   - Historical Gxx provenance MUST disappear from current executable qualification.
   - Active test files named test_gXX_* = 0.
   - Active tool files named gXX_* = 0.
   - Current qualification decisions based on Gxx identity/history = 0.
   - Historical evidence remains in Git/evidence/provenance only and MUST NOT execute as a release
     universe.

4. DEPENDENCY-REDUCTION GATE
   - Dependency edges among product, tests, release engineering, docs and evidence MUST materially
     decrease.
   - Behavioral and Integration tests MUST NOT read canonical docs, historical evidence, release
     receipts or Gxx history to decide product correctness.
   - Source/AST inspection is limited to the durable GS04 architectural invariants and MUST NOT
     become a distributed substitute for behavioral tests.
   - Final GS07 evidence MUST include dependency-edge before/after counts or an equivalently precise
     machine-auditable dependency ledger.

5. HARNESS-SIZE GATE
   - Runner, harness, release-verifier and support orchestration MUST shrink materially.
   - GS07 MUST NOT replace the old Gxx tool collection with a new sophisticated generic framework of
     comparable size or coupling.
   - Shared infrastructure is permitted only when it removes duplication and has one narrow owner.
   - If the new permanent harness begins recreating old orchestration complexity, GS07 MUST STOP and
     be redesigned before cutover.

6. TEST-VALUE GATE
   - Every active test MUST protect a current behavior, integration contract, desktop property,
     performance property or durable architecture invariant.
   - Exact prose, receipt markers, historical current-work-item identity, exact test counts, method
     names, incidental import order and implementation literals are NOT valid qualification targets
     except where a narrowly defined durable boundary genuinely requires source inspection.
   - A rewritten replacement MUST demonstrate negative-fixture/fault sensitivity before the older
     oracle can be retired.

7. NO-COMPATIBILITY-SHIM GATE
   - Compatibility wrappers that keep retired Gxx validation executable under new semantic names are
     FORBIDDEN.
   - New authorities MUST NOT import, invoke, shell out to, delegate to or require retired Gxx
     validation tools/tests after cutover.
   - During GS07 only, the exact published-G09 legacy system may exist in a physically separate
     shadow copy solely for equivalence comparison. It is not part of the active rebaseline tree.

8. PACKAGING-SEPARATION GATE
   - The installed/runtime Graphium product MUST contain product/runtime/user-facing resources, not
     the certification laboratory.
   - Active tests, shadow harnesses, historical evidence bundles and certification-only support tools
     MUST NOT be installed as normal product runtime payload, except for a specifically justified
     minimal smoke surface.

9. PRODUCT/RELEASE-SEPARATION GATE
   - Changing Candidate, R1/R2, attempt count, commit, tree, publication receipt or release state
     MUST NOT change a single runtime Graphium byte.
   - Gxx work-item identity, Candidate identity and release-engineering metadata MUST be absent from
     runtime product identity.
   - Product version/application identity may remain product properties; validation lineage may not.

10. COMPLEXITY-TRAJECTORY GATE
    - Each simplification stage MUST publish before/after measurements.
    - At minimum measure: active validation LOC, active validation files, harness/tool LOC, authority
      nodes, executable historical nodes, source-reading tests, docs/evidence-reading tests,
      compatibility surfaces and dependency edges.
    - The structural trajectory MUST decline clearly. A 5-10% cosmetic reduction is not sufficient
      where historical duplication remains materially intact.

GS07 GRAPHium-SPECIFIC ZERO TARGETS
-----------------------------------
Before GS07 may close:

  ACTIVE_GXX_NAMED_TEST_FILES=0
  ACTIVE_GXX_NAMED_TOOL_FILES=0
  ACTIVE_VERIFY_GRAPHIUM_PY=0
  GENERATION_SPECIFIC_TRUE_GTK_FRAMEWORKS=0
  DOCS_EVIDENCE_PROSE_ORACLES=0
  EXACT_TEST_COUNT_ORACLES=0
  RUNTIME_GXX_CANDIDATE_RELEASE_METADATA=0
  RETIRED_GXX_COMPATIBILITY_IMPORTS_OR_WRAPPERS=0
  TESTS_READING_HISTORICAL_DOCS_OR_EVIDENCE=0
  STRUCTURAL_SOURCE_INSPECTION_INVARIANTS<=10

DELETION PROOF — REQUIRED
-------------------------
Every structural simplification stage MUST provide a deletion proof, not merely a migration report.
For GS07 the final proof MUST include:

  - physical file-level deletion ledger: what was deleted;
  - LOC before/after for active validation and orchestration;
  - authority-node before/after ledger;
  - dependency-edge before/after ledger;
  - executable-Gxx-history before/after ledger;
  - source-reading and docs/evidence-reading test before/after counts;
  - retired compatibility surfaces, with final count zero;
  - active-vs-shadow separation proof;
  - 100% old-property -> permanent-owner coverage map;
  - replacement fault/negative-fixture sensitivity proof;
  - proof that deleted infrastructure is not required by any permanent authority;
  - packaging separation proof;
  - runtime/release metadata separation proof.

A list of renamed files is NOT a deletion proof.
A smaller line count without authority/dependency reduction is NOT a deletion proof.
Passing all tests without this proof is NOT sufficient.

ANTI-COSMETIC EXAMPLES
----------------------
INVALID / COSMETIC:
  20 historical gXX_* tools -> 20 semantic wrappers around the same tools;
  test_gXX_* files renamed while retaining the same history/literal meta-oracles;
  VERIFY_GRAPHIUM.py split into multiple modules with the same Gxx/document/evidence knowledge;
  old Gxx infrastructure retained behind a compatibility dispatcher;
  release metadata moved to another runtime module rather than removed from runtime.

VALID / STRUCTURAL:
  historical regression universes -> current behavioral/integration/desktop properties with one
  owner each;
  one shared narrow GTK harness replacing duplicated generation-specific harness machinery;
  historical evidence retained as non-executable provenance;
  source-shape checking reduced to the small durable boundary set;
  obsolete profiles/tools/tests physically deleted after coverage and fault-sensitivity proof;
  Candidate/commit/tree/attempt state confined entirely to external release engineering.

STOP / REDESIGN RULE
--------------------
If GS07 or any later simplification requires a new framework sufficiently complex to recreate the
old orchestration, meta-oracle, manifest/profile or compatibility burden, the line MUST STOP before
cutover and be redesigned. Graphium MUST NOT replace historical validation pathology with a more
polished or generically named validation pathology.

CUTOVER / G10 UNFREEZE RULE
---------------------------
G10 may be unfrozen ONLY when ALL of the following are true on the exact final GS07 rebaseline:

  OLD_G09_SHADOW_FUNCTIONAL_EQUIVALENCE=PASS
  NEW_BEHAVIORAL_AUTHORITY=PASS
  NEW_INTEGRATION_AUTHORITY=PASS
  NEW_TRUE_GTK_DESKTOP_AUTHORITY=PASS
  NEW_PACKAGING_RELEASE_AUTHORITY=PASS
  DELETION_PROOF=PASS
  AUTHORITY_COUNT_REDUCTION=PASS
  DEPENDENCY_REDUCTION=PASS
  NO_COMPATIBILITY_SHIMS=PASS
  PACKAGING_SEPARATION=PASS
  PRODUCT_RELEASE_SEPARATION=PASS
  COMPLEXITY_TRAJECTORY=PASS
  STRUCTURAL_SIMPLIFICATION_IS_NET_REDUCTIVE=PASS

If any one condition is FAIL, STOP or unproven:
  GS07=CANNOT_CLOSE
  G10_FROZEN=YES
  G10_CANDIDATE_ATTEMPTS=0/2

This entry is binding governance and may not be weakened merely to make GS07, G10 or any later
qualification pass. Any future relaxation requires a separate source-grounded audit, mature-source
comparison and explicit user authorization.

G11 STAGE 2 — STRONG LIVE MONITOR
---------------------------------
Frozen architecture:
  Gio.FileMonitor events are interrupt/wakeup signals only.
  They are NEVER accepted disk truth.

Strong truth remains:
  observe_document()
  +
  classify_fresh_observation()

Strong observation owns:
  logical-path identity;
  canonical/resolved object observation;
  device/inode identity;
  complete SHA-256 content fingerprint;
  size/mtime/ctime/mode/uid/gid/nlink facts;
  stable-before/stable-after observation;
  fail-closed unstable/torn evidence handling.

GuardedFileWriter remains:
  the sole physical writer;
  the sole ordinary-Save safety authority.

Monitoring invariants:
  PERIODIC_POLLING=NO
  IDLE_BACKGROUND_HASHING=NO
  AUTO_RELOAD=NO
  MAX_CONCURRENT_STRONG_OBSERVATIONS=1
  UNBOUNDED_QUEUE=NO
  GENERIC_JOB_FRAMEWORK=NO
  MONITOR_ACCEPTED_BASELINE_MUTATION=NO
  EVENT_AS_TRUTH=NO

Lifecycle:
  suspend/rebind around New/Open/Open Recent/direct open/Save/Save As/Reload/Close.
  stale lifecycle-generation results must never reach the UI.

Symlink:
  direct symlink-opened document monitors:
    accepted resolved target
    +
    logical symlink path topology through a narrowly-scoped parent-directory monitor.

External-state UI:
  one low-noise nonmodal Gtk.InfoBar;
  no repeated modal warning storm;
  no automatic reload;
  Reload button only where semantically applicable.
